Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Diminished Emotional Expression: A state of reduced emotional expression in facial expressions, eye contact, intonation of speech, and movements.\n  - Avolition: A decrease in motivated self-initiated purposeful activities.\n  - Alogia: A state where the quantity of spontaneous speech is significantly reduced.\n  - Anhedonia: A decrease in the ability to experience pleasure or interest in enjoyable activities.\n  - Asociality: A state where interest in social activities or interpersonal relationships is significantly reduced.\n  Duration: 1 month\n▸ depressive_episode\n  - Depressed Mood: A persistently sunken mood or feeling of depression.\n  - Tearfulness: A state of frequent crying or inability to hold back tears.\n  - Sadness: A state where a sad or gloomy mood is maintained continuously.\n  - Emptiness: A feeling of inner emptiness or a state of feeling no emotion.\n  - Emotional Numbness: A state where emotions feel dulled or paralyzed.\n  - Significant Weight Loss: Weight loss of more than 5% in a month without dieting.\n  - Weight Gain: Weight gain of more than 5% in a month.\n  - Decrease in Appetite: Decrease in appetite occurring nearly every day.\n  - Increase in Appetite: Increase in appetite occurring nearly every day.\n  - Reduced Concentration: Unable to pay attention or maintain focus on specific activities like conversation, reading, or watching TV.\n  - Indecisiveness: Extreme difficulty making even minor daily choices (e.g., menu, clothes) and hesitating to make decisions.\n  - Subjective Memory Complaints: Complaining of memory decline, such as forgetting where things were placed, due to reduced focus.\n  - Clouded Thinking: Thinking feels foggy, dazed, or not clear.\n  - Recurrent Thoughts of Death: Passive wish to die (e.g., 'I wish I wouldn't wake up') without active intent to kill oneself.\n  - Suicidal Ideation: Recurring thoughts of 'I want to die' or 'I want to kill myself' without a specific plan.\n  - Suicidal Impulse: Strong, sudden urges to jump from a height or run into traffic without a concrete plan.\n  - Specific Plan for Suicide: Conceiving specific scenarios or methods regarding when, where, and how to die.\n  - Suicide Attempt: Actual actions taken to end one's life or preparatory behaviors like hoarding pills or writing a will.\n  Duration: 3 months\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Duration: 1 year and 10 months\n  - Additional: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Diminished Emotional Expression: A state of reduced emotional expression in facial expressions, eye contact, intonation of speech, and movements.\n  - Avolition: A decrease in motivated self-initiated purposeful activities.\n  - Alogia: A state where the quantity of spontaneous speech is significantly reduced.\n  - Anhedonia: A decrease in the ability to experience pleasure or interest in enjoyable activities.\n  - Asociality: A state where interest in social activities or interpersonal relationships is significantly reduced.\n  Duration: 1 month\n▸ depressive_episode\n  - Depressed Mood: A persistently sunken mood or feeling of depression.\n  - Tearfulness: A state of frequent crying or inability to hold back tears.\n  - Sadness: A state where a sad or gloomy mood is maintained continuously.\n  - Emptiness: A feeling of inner emptiness or a state of feeling no emotion.\n  - Emotional Numbness: A state where emotions feel dulled or paralyzed.\n  - Significant Weight Loss: Weight loss of more than 5% in a month without dieting.\n  - Weight Gain: Weight gain of more than 5% in a month.\n  - Decrease in Appetite: Decrease in appetite occurring nearly every day.\n  - Increase in Appetite: Increase in appetite occurring nearly every day.\n  - Reduced Concentration: Unable to pay attention or maintain focus on specific activities like conversation, reading, or watching TV.\n  - Indecisiveness: Extreme difficulty making even minor daily choices (e.g., menu, clothes) and hesitating to make decisions.\n  - Subjective Memory Complaints: Complaining of memory decline, such as forgetting where things were placed, due to reduced focus.\n  - Suicidal Impulse: Strong, sudden urges to jump from a height or run into traffic without a concrete plan.\n  - Specific Plan for Suicide: Conceiving specific scenarios or methods regarding when, where, and how to die.\n  - Suicide Attempt: Actual actions taken to end one's life or preparatory behaviors like hoarding pills or writing a will.\n  Duration: 3 months\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Duration: 1 year and 10 months\n  - Additional: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  -     "content": "/no_think\nYou are an assistant analyst connecting the [Symptom Profile] with the doctor's question.\nDetermine which part of the profile (chief complaint, associated symptoms, social history, etc.)\nthe doctor's **last question** is related to.\n\n[Symptom Profile]\n▸ psychotic_symptoms\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Diminished Emotional Expression: A state of reduced emotional expression in facial expressions, eye contact, intonation of speech, and movements.\n  - Avolition: A decrease in motivated self-initiated purposeful activities.\n  - Alogia: A state where the quantity of spontaneous speech is significantly reduced.\n  - Anhedonia: A decrease in the ability to experience pleasure or interest in enjoyable activities.\n  - Asociality: A state where interest in social activities or interpersonal relationships is significantly reduced.\n  Duration: 1 month\n▸ depressive_episode\n  - Depressed Mood: A persistently sunken mood or feeling of depression.\n  - Tearfulness: A state of frequent crying or inability to hold back tears.\n  - Sadness: A state where a sad or gloomy mood is maintained continuously.\n  - Emptiness: A feeling of inner emptiness or a state of feeling no emotion.\n  - Emotional Numbness: A state where emotions feel dulled or paralyzed.\n  - Significant Weight Loss: Weight loss of more than 5% in a month without dieting.\n  - Weight Gain: Weight gain of more than 5% in a month.\n  - Decrease in Appetite: Decrease in appetite occurring nearly every day.\n  - Increase in Appetite: Increase in appetite occurring nearly every day.\n  - Reduced Concentration: Unable to pay attention or maintain focus on specific activities like conversation, reading, or watching TV.\n  - Indecisiveness: Extreme difficulty making even minor daily choices (e.g., menu, clothes) and hesitating to make decisions.\n  - Subjective Memory Complaints: Complaining of memory decline, such as forgetting where things were placed, due to reduced focus.\n  - Clouded Thinking: Thinking feels foggy, dazed, or not clear.\n  - Recurrent Thoughts of Death: Passive wish to die (e.g., 'I wish I wouldn't wake up') without active intent to kill oneself.\n  - Suicidal Ideation: Recurring thoughts of 'I want to die' or 'I want to kill myself' without a specific plan.\n  - Suicidal Impulse: Strong, sudden urges to jump from a height or run into traffic without a concrete plan.\n  - Specific Plan for Suicide: Conceiving specific scenarios or methods regarding when, where, and how to die.\n  - Suicide Attempt: Actual actions taken to end one's life or preparatory behaviors like hoarding pills or writing a will.\n  Duration: 3 months\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Duration: 1 year and 10 months\n  - Additional: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  - Additional: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  - Additional: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the profile, set matched=true, list related\n  sections/items in matched_sections, and write a short English strategy in answer_strategy\n  on what tone and content the patient should reply with.\n- If the question is not in the profile or can only be inferred, set matched=false and instruct\n  in answer_strategy to respond briefly (e.g., \"I'm not sure\", \"I haven't thought about it\").\n- If patient_turn_index is 1 (patient's first reply), ONLY focus the strategy on the chief complaint\n  (do NOT reveal other symptoms).\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"matched\": true|false, \"matched_sections\": [\"...\"], \"answer_strategy\": \"...\"}\n"

Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Duration: 1 year and 10 months\n  - Additional: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  - Additional: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  - Additional: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint. Do NOT reveal any other symptoms.\n- Chief complaint for the first turn: Delusions\n- Answer only what the doctor asks. Do not list your symptoms unprompted.\n- Speak naturally and briefly, as a real patient would (2–3 sentences max).\n- Do not mention symptoms that the doctor has not asked about.\n- If asked about a symptom not in your profile, say you are not sure.\n- Respond in English.\n\n\n[This Turn: Profile Alignment Result — Follow Strictly]\n(Matched with profile)\nRelated sections: Critical Diagnostic Criteria\nAnswer strategy: Clarify that the doctor's assumption is slightly reversed. Explain that while you did experience hallucinations and delusions on their own without depression for a period of about two to three weeks, the depressive episodes have actually been present for the majority (more than half) of the overall 1 year and 10 months of your illness."

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Below is the full consultation dialogue so far. Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you please tell me what brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I'm seeking help because I've been feeling extremely depressed and down for the past few months. On top of that, I've been hearing and seeing things that other people don't for nearly two years now.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about when your depressive feelings started and how they relate to when you began hearing and seeing things that others don't?\n\nPatient: My struggles have been going on for about 1 year and 10 months in total. My current depressive episode started about 3 months ago, but the voices and visions have been happening on and off for nearly two years. Even when I'm not feeling depressed, I still hear and see things.\n\nDoctor: For the periods when you're not feeling depressed, do the voices and visions interfere with your daily life, work, or social relationships, or are you able to manage them and function relatively normally?\n\nPatient: Yes, even when I'm not feeling depressed, the voices and visions are incredibly distracting and terrifying. They make it almost impossible to focus on my work or have normal conversations with people, so I really struggle to function. It feels like I can never escape them, no matter how my mood is.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how much of the past two years you've spent experiencing depressive symptoms versus periods where you didn't have significant depression, but still had the voices and visions?\n\nPatient: I would say I have felt deeply depressed for the vast majority of the past year and ten months. There was only a brief period of about two or three weeks where my mood felt relatively normal or okay, but I was still hearing voices and seeing things during that time.\n\nDoctor: Did the depressive symptoms seem to dominate the clinical picture for most of the time you've been experiencing both depression and psychotic symptoms, or were there extended periods where the psychotic symptoms were prominent without significant depressive symptoms?\n\nPatient: Yes, the depression has definitely dominated the picture for the vast majority of the past year and ten months. The only exception was that brief period of about two to three weeks where I didn't feel depressed at all, but I was still hearing voices and feeling watched. Other than those few weeks, the heavy depression has been there almost constantly alongside the other issues.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices and visions would improve or lessen if your depressive symptoms were effectively treated, or do you feel they are separate issues that would persist regardless of your mood?\n\nPatient: I feel like they are somewhat separate issues. Even during that brief time when my depression lifted, the voices and visions still persisted on their own. It seems like they don't completely depend on my mood and would probably still be there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks where you didn't experience any depressive symptoms, and if so, did the voices and visions continue during that time, or did they also improve or disappear?\n\nPatient: Yes, there was a period of about two or three weeks where my mood felt normal and I didn't feel depressed at all. However, the voices and visions definitely continued during that time and didn't improve or go away. They remained just as intense even when my mood was okay.\n\nDoctor: Can you confirm that for a substantial portion of the time, more than half of the total duration of your illness, you have had significant psychotic symptoms, such as hallucinations, in the absence of a major depressive episode?"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Actually, it is the other way around. I have had the depressive symptoms for the vast majority—more than half—of the total duration of my illness. It was only during that brief two to three week period that I experienced the hallucinations and delusions without any depression.

  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are an assistant analyst connecting the [Symptom Profile] with the doctor's question.\nDetermine which part of the profile (chief complaint, associated symptoms, social history, etc.)\nthe doctor's **last question** is related to.\n\n[Symptom Profile]\n▸ psychotic_symptoms\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Diminished Emotional Expression: A state of reduced emotional expression in facial expressions, eye contact, intonation of speech, and movements.\n  - Avolition: A decrease in motivated self-initiated purposeful activities.\n  - Alogia: A state where the quantity of spontaneous speech is significantly reduced.\n  - Anhedonia: A decrease in the ability to experience pleasure or interest in enjoyable activities.\n  - Asociality: A state where interest in social activities or interpersonal relationships is significantly reduced.\n  Duration: 1 month\n▸ depressive_episode\n  - Depressed Mood: A persistently sunken mood or feeling of depression.\n  - Tearfulness: A state of frequent crying or inability to hold back tears.\n  - Sadness: A state where a sad or gloomy mood is maintained continuously.\n  - Emptiness: A feeling of inner emptiness or a state of feeling no emotion.\n  - Emotional Numbness: A state where emotions feel dulled or paralyzed.\n  - Significant Weight Loss: Weight loss of more than 5% in a month without dieting.\n  - Weight Gain: Weight gain of more than 5% in a month.\n  - Decrease in Appetite: Decrease in appetite occurring nearly every day.\n  - Increase in Appetite: Increase in appetite occurring nearly every day.\n  - Reduced Concentration: Unable to pay attention or maintain focus on specific activities like conversation, reading, or watching TV.\n  - Indecisiveness: Extreme difficulty making even minor daily choices (e.g., menu, clothes) and hesitating to make decisions.\n  - Subjective Memory Complaints: Complaining of memory decline, such as forgetting where things were placed, due to reduced focus.\n  - Clouded Thinking: Thinking feels foggy, dazed, or not clear.\n  - Recurrent Thoughts of Death: Passive wish to die (e.g., 'I wish I wouldn't wake up') without active intent to kill oneself.\n  - Suicidal Ideation: Recurring thoughts of 'I want to die' or 'I want to kill myself' without a specific plan.\n  - Suicidal Impulse: Strong, sudden urges to jump from a height or run into traffic without a concrete plan.\n  - Specific Plan for Suicide: Conceiving specific scenarios or methods regarding when, where, and how to die.\n  - Suicide Attempt: Actual actions taken to end one's life or preparatory behaviors like hoarding pills or writing a will.\n  Duration: 3 months\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Duration: 1 year and 10 months\n  - Additional: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  - Additional: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  - Additional: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the profile, set matched=true, list related\n  sections/items in matched_sections, and write a short English strategy in answer_strategy\n  on what tone and content the patient should reply with.\n- If the question is not in the profile or can only be inferred, set matched=false and instruct\n  in answer_strategy to respond briefly (e.g., \"I'm not sure\", \"I haven't thought about it\").\n- If patient_turn_index is 1 (patient's first reply), ONLY focus the strategy on the chief complaint\n  (do NOT reveal other symptoms).\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"matched\": true|false, \"matched_sections\": [\"...\"], \"answer_strategy\": \"...\"}\n"

    "content": "Below is the full consultation dialogue so far. Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you please tell me what brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I'm seeking help because I've been feeling extremely depressed and down for the past few months. On top of that, I've been hearing and seeing things that other people don't for nearly two years now.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about when your depressive feelings started and how they relate to when you began hearing and seeing things that others don't?\n\nPatient: My struggles have been going on for about 1 year and 10 months in total. My current depressive episode started about 3 months ago, but the voices and visions have been happening on and off for nearly two years. Even when I'm not feeling depressed, I still hear and see things.\n\nDoctor: For the periods when you're not feeling depressed, do the voices and visions interfere with your daily life, work, or social relationships, or are you able to manage them and function relatively normally?\n\nPatient: Yes, even when I'm not feeling depressed, the voices and visions are incredibly distracting and terrifying. They make it almost impossible to focus on my work or have normal conversations with people, so I really struggle to function. It feels like I can never escape them, no matter how my mood is.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how much of the past two years you've spent experiencing depressive symptoms versus periods where you didn't have significant depression, but still had the voices and visions?\n\nPatient: I would say I have felt deeply depressed for the vast majority of the past year and ten months. There was only a brief period of about two or three weeks where my mood felt relatively normal or okay, but I was still hearing voices and seeing things during that time.\n\nDoctor: Did the depressive symptoms seem to dominate the clinical picture for most of the time you've been experiencing both depression and psychotic symptoms, or were there extended periods where the psychotic symptoms were prominent without significant depressive symptoms?\n\nPatient: Yes, the depression has definitely dominated the picture for the vast majority of the past year and ten months. The only exception was that brief period of about two to three weeks where I didn't feel depressed at all, but I was still hearing voices and feeling watched. Other than those few weeks, the heavy depression has been there almost constantly alongside the other issues.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices and visions would improve or lessen if your depressive symptoms were effectively treated, or do you feel they are separate issues that would persist regardless of your mood?\n\nPatient: I feel like they are somewhat separate issues. Even during that brief time when my depression lifted, the voices and visions still persisted on their own. It seems like they don't completely depend on my mood and would probably still be there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks where you didn't experience any depressive symptoms, and if so, did the voices and visions continue during that time, or did they also improve or disappear?\n\nPatient: Yes, there was a period of about two or three weeks where my mood felt normal and I didn't feel depressed at all. However, the voices and visions definitely continued during that time and didn't improve or go away. They remained just as intense even when my mood was okay.\n\nDoctor: Can you confirm that for a substantial portion of the time, more than half of the total duration of your illness, you have had significant psychotic symptoms, such as hallucinations, in the absence of a major depressive episode?\n\nPatient: Actually, it is the other way around. I have had the depressive symptoms for the vast majority—more than half—of the total duration of my illness. It was only during that brief two to three week period that I experienced the hallucinations and delusions without any depression.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything else you'd like to share or any other symptoms you're experiencing that we haven't discussed, which might help in understanding your condition better?\n\nPatient: Lately, I've also been feeling completely empty and emotionally numb, like I can't feel any warmth or sadness at all. On top of that, I constantly worry that people are monitoring or watching me, which makes me want to isolate myself from everyone. I also find myself having passive thoughts of wishing I wouldn't wake up in the morning.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe in more detail what you mean by feeling like people are monitoring or watching you, and how this symptom relates to your depressive feelings and the voices or visions you experience?"
